The phone and tablet steps list is redesigned: compact rows with icons, a progress line that stops exactly at the active step, and each step's visual inside its open card. Lint, TypeScript and every check below pass on production builds. Nothing is committed or pushed.

**About your screenshot:** it shows the desktop layout's left column (1024px and up) at the end of its scroll, not the phone or tablet accordion. Desktop spreads the rows to fill the panel height, and the line was full because scrolling had reached 100%. The accordion below 1024px wasn't stretched, but I made all the requested changes there anyway.

**What changed below 1024px**
- **Compact rows:** each row sizes to its content. Collapsed rows are 72px tall with 10px gaps, and there's no stretching.
- **Row layout:** an icon chip, the title (darker than before, about 9:1 contrast), a short one-line teaser, and the step number in mono at the right. The teaser folds away when the row opens.
- **Icons:** Read uses file-code, Predict crosshair, Modify pencil-line, Verify shield-check, and Revisit rotate-ccw.
  - **Active:** an orange tint with a soft glow.
  - **Completed:** a green check, since orange never means success.
  - **Upcoming:** neutral.
- **Progress line:** a 2px line runs through the chip centres. The orange fill runs from the first chip to the active one and animates in 400ms; reduced motion makes it instant.
- **Open card:** the description, then a compact visual that fades in slightly after the card opens. Read shows 5 lines of code, Predict shows 3 options, and the others reuse the desktop visuals. Sample data is labelled. Code stays at 13px and scrolls sideways inside its window when needed.
- **Interaction:**
  - Rows are tap-driven, and only one is open at a time; tapping it again closes it.
  - Enter or Space toggles a row, and the arrow keys move between rows.
  - A newly opened row scrolls into view just below the nav, at 92px.
- **Desktop:** only the same icon chips were added, plus the darker inactive titles from the contrast fix.

**Measured at 390, 360, 320 and 768px, with steps 1, 3 and 5 open**
- **Progress line:** the fill ends exactly on the active chip's centre at all four widths. At 390 that's 269.3px versus 269.3px with step 3 open, and 128.3 versus 128.3 with step 5. The line passes through every chip centre.
- **Spacing:** collapsed rows are 72px tall, and every gap between them is 10px.
- **Stability:** opening a step never moves the rows above it.
- **Rest:** no horizontal overflow and no console errors at any width.
- **Accessibility:** tap targets are 68px tall. Reduced motion makes every state change instant, and with JavaScript off all five steps are readable.
- **Desktop:** the sticky behaviour still passes everything except layout shift while switching steps. That measures 0.012 at 1280 and 0.026 at 1024, still well under the 0.1 budget, from the same accordion expansion as before.

**Still different from your description**
- **Teasers are shorter** (5 or 6 words, such as "Commit to an outcome first.") instead of about 12, so they fit one line on phones. They fit fully at 390 and 768. At 360, "Same idea, new code, fewer hints." ends in "…", and at 320 four of them do.
- **Line visibility in open cards:** the grey line shows in the open card's left margin, and the code windows cover it where they sit.

**Files changed:** [sticky-steps.tsx](src/components/marketing/sticky-steps.tsx), [learning-loop.tsx](src/components/marketing/learning-loop.tsx) and [globals.css](src/app/globals.css). The earlier, still-uncommitted changes from the last round (the copy button, code-window.tsx, and the CLAUDE.md update) are also still in the working tree.

I fixed this, although the error came from your React Developer Tools extension rather than Trace's code. Every frame in the stack is inside that extension. With React DevTools 8.0.0 loaded in a test browser, browsing the page produced the error twice before the fix and 0 times after, in both your dev server and a production build.

**Cause:** DevTools throws "The children should not have changed if we pass in the same set" when one server-rendered element appears in two places in the client tree. React allows that, but DevTools' tree tracking can't handle it. Two parts of the page did this:
- **Learning Loop steps:** each step's visual element was shown both in the desktop panel and in the phone/tablet accordion.
- **Magnifier lens:** the code element was shown both in the code window and inside the lens.

**Fix:** each of those places now gets its own separately created element. The output is identical; only the duplication is gone.
- [code-window.tsx](src/components/code/code-window.tsx): a separate code copy for the lens.
- [sticky-steps.tsx](src/components/marketing/sticky-steps.tsx): the accordion visual is now a required, separate field, with no fallback to the shared one.
- [learning-loop.tsx](src/components/marketing/learning-loop.tsx): the Modify, Verify and Revisit visuals are built twice from small functions, once for the desktop panel and once for the accordion.

**Checked:** lint and TypeScript pass. The lens, the desktop sticky steps and the accordion suites behave as before. The only failing check is the known 0.012–0.026 layout shift when switching steps on desktop, under the 0.1 budget.

One caveat: Chrome now blocks loading extensions from the command line, so I reproduced it with the same extension in Edge rather than Chrome. It's the same extension code.

Nothing is committed or pushed.

Two refinements to the Trace marketing site. Keep the approved visual design. Don't touch the backend, the real lesson logic, or the verification system. Don't run git push, and don't commit unless I ask.

## Part 1: Make all examples simple, and use JavaScript

Goal: a beginner who builds with AI agents should understand every code sample and every piece of sample data in a few seconds. Use simple things mostly. All displayed code on the marketing site is JavaScript (Node + Express), not Python.

**One shared example.** Use the same tiny example across the whole homepage (hero showcase, feature cards, steps section, flow diagram, exercise walkthrough, learning history) so people learn one idea deeply. Proposed example:

```js
const express = require("express");
const app = express();
app.use(express.json());

const notes = [];

app.post("/notes", (req, res) => {
  const title = req.body?.title;
  if (!title) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: "title is required" });
  }
  notes.push(title);
  res.status(201).json({ saved: true });
});
```

- Before using it, verify it is correct Express behavior: run it in the workspace if you can (install express temporarily outside the project, or in a temp folder, not as a project dependency), and send a request with no title and one with a title. Confirm the status codes and the contents of `notes`. If anything differs, fix the example and tell me. Also verify that `req.body?.title` works whether `req.body` is `{}` or `undefined` on the Express version you test.
- About 12 lines, with no database, no routers, no validation libraries, no TypeScript, no async/await, and no extra imports.
- The prediction question: "A request arrives without a `title`. What does the API return, and what is in `notes` afterwards?" The answer: status `400`, and `notes` is still empty, because the function returns before `notes.push`.
- The modify step: a tiny, obvious change, for example returning how many notes exist: `res.status(201).json({ saved: true, count: notes.length })`.
- The verify step: a small evidence panel: request body, HTTP status, response body, and the contents of `notes` (for example `[]` after the failed request, `["Buy milk"]` after a good one). Label it "Sample result" wherever it isn't live from the backend.
- Revisit: the same idea in different code, for example a `POST /tasks` route that needs a `name` field.
- Update every code label and file name to match: for example `server.js`, language badge `JAVASCRIPT · EXPRESS`, and flow-diagram labels (`POST /notes` → `express.json() reads the body` → `check title` → `notes.push(title)` → `201 Created`, with a branch to `400 Bad Request` when title is missing). Switch syntax highlighting to JavaScript. The "agent-generated, under review" framing can stay.
- Update CLAUDE.md wherever it says "Python" or "FastAPI example" for homepage code samples (section 5, sections 6 and 12 if relevant), so it says "simple JavaScript (Node + Express) example". Note in CLAUDE.md that the platform backend remains FastAPI and that only the displayed lesson snippets on the marketing site are JavaScript. Show me the diff.
- If the real lesson workspace uses different code or another language, leave it alone. Just list for me which lessons you think are too complex for beginners, and flag if a displayed JavaScript example cannot be verified live by the current backend.

**Sample data rules.** Use friendly, obvious values: "Buy milk", "Call mom", "title", "name". No random hashes, long JSON blobs, UUIDs, or realistic-looking but meaningless data. Keep any JSON to 1-3 short fields. No numbers that look like statistics.

**Code readability rules.**
- Short lines (under about 60 characters), consistent 2-space indentation (standard for JavaScript), meaningful names (`createNote`-style naming is fine, but avoid `x` or `tmp`).
- At most one new concept per code sample. Highlight the single line that matters for that step.
- Add one short plain-language caption under or beside each code sample, for example "This line checks the request. If `title` is missing, the function stops here and sends back 400."
- Use the magnifier targets to point at only 2-3 lines per sample, each with a short plain label.

**Copy rules (all marketing text).**
- Short sentences, everyday words, 8th-grade reading level. Explain any term the first time it appears, in a few words (for example "HTTP status (a number that says what happened)").
- Replace jargon where possible: "validation" becomes "checks the input", "persist" becomes "save".
- Keep all positioning rules from CLAUDE.md (Trace is a learning app, not an AI agent; no unsupported claims; no fake stats).
- Don't change the hero headline, eyebrow, or CTAs unless they conflict with these rules.

After the changes, give me a short table: where each example appeared before, what it shows now, and the files changed.

## Part 2: Smooth card animations on scroll

Apply to the three feature cards and any other card grids (principles, learning history, steps visuals, flow cards).

- **Entrance (on scroll into view, once):** each card starts at opacity 0, translateY 40px, scale 0.97, and resolves to opacity 1, translateY 0, scale 1 over about 800ms with our entrance easing, `cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1)`. Skip blur on cards unless it costs no performance. Stagger 100-140ms between cards (left to right). Trigger when the card's top reaches about 88% of the viewport height.
- **Inner content follows:** after each card lands, its inner visual reveals in a second, softer pass (about 300ms delay): code lines fade in top to bottom (30-40ms apart), diff rows appear one by one, evidence rows slide up slightly. Keep each inner animation under 600ms total.
- **Gentle scroll-linked depth (desktop only, subtle):** while a card is on screen, its inner visual drifts up or down by at most 12-16px relative to the card (a small parallax tied to scroll progress, scrub about 0.5). The card frame itself does not move. This gives a smooth, alive feel without being distracting.
- **Hover (desktop only):** lift 4-6px over 200ms, shadow softens, inner visual scales 1.02. No tilt and no glow borders.
- **Rules:** use transform and opacity only (no animating layout properties). Once triggered, never replay on scroll back. No layout shift: cards reserve their space before animating. With JS disabled, cards are visible in their final state. Reduced motion: no movement, just a quick 150ms opacity fade. Mobile: the same entrance but with a 24px rise, no parallax, and a shorter stagger (60-80ms). Use the shared Reveal component and motion tokens already in `lib/motion`. Don't create ad-hoc values. Use Motion for React or CSS for these. Don't add new libraries.

## Verification (don't claim anything you didn't run)
- Run `next build && next start`. Scroll through the page at 1440x900, 768, and 390x844, and capture mid-animation frames of a card grid (card 1 landed, card 2 mid-reveal, card 3 not started).
- Confirm that: the same simple JavaScript example appears consistently in every section with correct JavaScript highlighting; every code sample is under about 14 lines; no leftover Python or FastAPI snippets or labels on the marketing pages; no horizontal overflow; no layout shift; reduced-motion works (emulate it); no console errors.
- Run lint and the TypeScript check. Report files changed, what was simplified, what you verified about the Express example, and anything you left alone on purpose.

| Learning-loop steps | Python per step | Read, Predict, Modify and Verify use `/notes`. Revisit uses `POST /tasks`, which needs a `name` | [learning-loop.tsx](src/components/marketing/learning-loop.tsx), [sticky-steps.tsx](src/components/marketing/sticky-steps.tsx) |
| Flow diagram | FastAPI request path | `POST /notes` → `express.json()` → `if (!title)` → 201 or 400 | [flows.ts](src/content/flows.ts), [flow-diagram.tsx](src/components/marketing/flow-diagram.tsx) |
| Walkthrough | FastAPI exercise | Six rows on the same route, ending with a plain-language "why" | [walkthrough.tsx](src/components/marketing/walkthrough.tsx) |

Copy changes are in [final-cta.tsx](src/components/marketing/final-cta.tsx). [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) has the new "Homepage example" and "Plain language" rules (the diff I showed earlier).

**What I simplified:**
- Every code window is 10 lines or fewer; the hero file is 15.
- The longest line is 55 characters.
- All 11 code windows have a caption.
- Sample data is "Buy milk" and JSON with 1–2 fields.
- No Python or FastAPI text is left on the page.

**What I checked about Express:** I ran the route on Express 5.3.0 and 4.22.3.
- No body, an empty body, or `{"title":""}` all return 400, and `notes` stays `[]`.
- With a title, it returns 201 and `notes` is `["Buy milk"]`.
- After the change, the reply is `{"saved":true,"count":1}`, then 2.
- `POST /tasks` behaves the same way.
- With no body, Express 5 leaves `req.body` undefined and Express 4 sets it to `{}`. `req.body?.title` handles both.

## Part 2: card animations

The timing values are in [tokens.ts](src/lib/motion/tokens.ts) and the logic is in [motion-runtime.tsx](src/components/motion/motion-runtime.tsx), plus [globals.css](src/app/globals.css) and [principles.tsx](src/components/marketing/principles.tsx). Measured in the browser:
- **Desktop and tablet:** cards start 117ms apart, rising 40px from 0.97 scale over 800ms. The inner rows follow about 300ms later.
- **Desktop parallax:** the visual inside each card drifts about 12px with scroll (measured 12.3px down to 5.3px). The card frame stays still.
- **Phones:** cards rise 24px, start about 67ms apart, and there's no parallax.
- **Hover:** cards lift 4px and the inside scales to 1.02.
- **Reduced motion:** cards fade in over 150ms with no movement.
- **No JavaScript:** every card shows in its final state.
- **Across all of it:** no layout shift (≤ 0.0001), no horizontal overflow, no console errors, and no React DevTools error.

## Things to know
- **The motion looks shorter than 800ms.** The easing is fast at the start, so cards are mostly in place by about 330–500ms. If you want it to feel slower, I can soften the easing for cards only.
- **Three checks failed at first because my test scripts were out of date.** They used the old button name, didn't wait long enough for the new row stagger, and expected reduced-motion cards to show before you scroll to them. I fixed the tests, not the site, and all three now pass.
- **Left alone on purpose:**
  - The flow diagram keeps its own node-by-node animation instead of the card entrance.
  - The hero file stays at 15 lines because the hero lesson needs the whole route.
- **There's no real lesson workspace in the repo yet**, so there were no complex lessons to list.
- **The backend can't run this JavaScript live.** The planned FastAPI backend doesn't exist yet and wouldn't run JavaScript anyway, so every result shown is labelled "Sample result".

To see the animation mid-way, open the screenshots at 140ms, e.g. `C:\Users\NexGen\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Users-NexGen-Desktop-trace\ac502e61-757d-4998-a421-8b1428fcceb3\scratchpad\qa\cards-1440-140.png`. The same file names exist for 768 and 390.

## Stage 1: Fix heading and section animations (find root causes first)

- `filter: blur` animated on many word elements at once. Replace the heading reveal with a line-mask reveal: each line in an `overflow: hidden` wrapper, words rising from `translateY(110%)` to 0 with a stagger, about 700ms, easing cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1). Use blur only (6px max) on short headings, never on the hero or long headings.

- Hydration flashes: content hidden before hydration or visible then hidden. The final state must be the default CSS state, with the hidden "from" state applied only after a JS-ready class.

## Heading animations: causes and fixes

- **Choppy reveals:** every heading word animated `filter: blur(12px)`, with a full-width overlay sweeping across.
  - **Fix:** each word now rises inside its own clipping mask, moving only (no blur, no overlay). Lines are read once after fonts load, so nothing re-splits or reflows.
  - The hero and the new `/learn` heading play from first paint without waiting for JavaScript.
  - **Measured:** I couldn't get a clean before/after number. The frame-timing tests at 4× slower CPU were too noisy, because other sections animate during the same scroll. The fix rests on removing the blur work, and the heading checks pass.
- **Already correct:** GSAP cleanup, refresh timing, play-once, the hidden-until-JavaScript-loads setup, and `will-change` handling all checked out. I left them alone.
- **Two things I didn't change:**
  - On a 4× slower CPU there's a 0.06–0.08 layout shift while the page's HTML is still loading. It comes from the hero's device mockup growing during load, not from any animation. That's under the 0.1 limit, and at normal speed it's 0.
  - The learning-loop steps shift by 0.03 at 1024px because the approved title list opens with a height animation. I confirmed it isn't from this round. It's also under 0.1, and removing it would mean redesigning that section.

## What's built

- **`/learn`, the lessons page:**
  - Status, locking and follow-ups come from the server.
  - Code previews come from each lesson's real code.
  - The "trace line" between lessons draws once, and the transfer lesson shows an unlock animation the first time it opens.
  - Also included: the guest banner, a "Continue where you left off" strip, loading placeholders, error-and-retry, and an honest "More lessons are being written" card.
  - **Important finding:** if a signed-in user opens a lesson, the backend permanently creates their account's learning record, and claiming this browser's guest history then fails. So `/learn` asks first: keep the guest progress, or start fresh.
- **Lesson pages:**
  - A breadcrumb back to Lessons.
  - A completion panel that appears only after the server confirms completion. It shows real facts and has "Next lesson" and "Back to lessons". After the last lesson it says when the first follow-up opens.
  - The only change to the lesson code is that the workspace now accepts `children`.
- **Navbar:**
  - "Lessons" added. Try a lesson, Try Trace and the final call to action all go to `/learn`.
  - "See how Trace works" now scrolls to the homepage demo.
  - Exactly one of "Sign in" or "Account" shows, and it updates without a reload.
- **Sign-in and sign-up:**
  - Split layout with original SVG/CSS art: the real lesson 1 code, labelled "Example", with a trace line and code lines that light up in turn. It pauses off-screen and stays still under reduced motion.
  - Form improvements: show/hide password, inline errors when leaving a field, a 12-character password rule, and a "Check your inbox" screen with a 30-second resend cooldown.
  - Behaviour: signed-in users are redirected to `/account`, and after sign-in users go to a safe `next` page (default `/learn`).
- **Google sign-in:** before redirecting, the button asks Supabase whether Google is enabled. If not, it shows "Google sign-in isn't set up on this site yet" instead of a blank page. Cancelled and disabled-provider errors also get friendly messages.
- **`/account`:**
  - A greeting, then real totals: lessons, attempts and hints.
  - A "How you learned" timeline with lines like "Request returned 422, nothing saved." and labels like "Verified after revision".
  - Follow-ups with an answer button, concepts practised, and the guest claim with a confirm step.

- **Docs:** [docs/auth-setup-checklist.md](docs/auth-setup-checklist.md) and [docs/lesson-proposals.md](docs/lesson-proposals.md) (six proposals, each with different code and a different bug type, clearly marked as not built). CLAUDE.md's motion notes are updated.

Correction to the heading animation work. I did NOT ask you to remove the blurred, frosted, word-by-word reveal. I asked you to make it smooth. Restore it, then fix the smoothness. Don't push, don't commit, and don't touch anything unrelated to heading and section animations.

## 1. Restore the original effect
Use `git diff` and `git log` (and any earlier version of `SplitHeading`) to see what the heading reveal looked like before the line-mask replacement. Bring back its visual behavior exactly:
- Words reveal one by one as the heading scrolls into view.
- Each word starts blurred, faded, and slightly lowered (about blur 12px, opacity 0, translateY 16-18px) and resolves to crisp (blur 0, opacity 1, translateY 0).
- A soft frosted, glassy feel while it resolves: a translucent gradient veil that sweeps across the line in the same direction as the stagger and disappears when done. It must leave no permanent glass effect behind.
- The accent word (for example "understand") resolves last with a slightly longer duration. A small icon chip beside a word, where it exists, scales in with a soft glow once.
- Plays once per heading when it reaches about 85% of the viewport. Applies to every section heading, and the hero headline uses the same effect on load.
Don't replace this effect with a mask or slide-up reveal. If a heading needs a line-mask for some reason, it may ADD a rise, but the blur-to-sharp resolve stays.

## 2. Make it smooth without removing it
Find the real causes of the choppiness and fix them. Test only in a production build (`next build && next start`) and use the browser's Performance panel (or Playwright tracing) to measure dropped frames during a heading reveal. Likely fixes, in this order:
- Fonts: wait for `document.fonts.ready` before splitting, split once, and don't re-split on resize unless line breaks actually change. No reflow while animating.
- Layer promotion: give each word `display: inline-block`, and apply `will-change: transform, opacity, filter` only for the duration of the reveal. Remove it on complete (so there are no permanent GPU layers).
- Fewer simultaneous blurred elements: cap how many words animate their blur at the same moment. For long headings (more than about 12 words), animate blur at the LINE level while words stagger with opacity and transform, so the effect looks the same but costs less.
- Animate with one timeline per heading (GSAP or Motion), not one effect per word. Avoid per-word React state and re-renders. Use refs and a single effect with cleanup (gsap.context and revert, which also handles React strict-mode double effects).
- Easing and timing tuned to feel soft: word duration about 0.8-1.0s, stagger 0.06-0.08s, ease cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1), and the blur resolving slightly slower than the opacity so words look like they are focusing.
- The veil: implement as one gradient-masked element per heading (transform or mask-position animation), not per word.
- Hydration: the final readable state is the default CSS state. The hidden starting state is applied only after a JS-ready class. No flash of visible-then-hidden text.
- ScrollTrigger: refresh after fonts and images load, make sure no pinned section changes layout height mid-reveal, and trigger once with no replay.
- Safari and low-power GPUs: if blur frames drop there, REDUCE the blur radius (for example 12px to 8px) or switch long headings to line-level blur. Do not remove the effect.
- Reduced motion: no blur, no movement, instant final state (this is the only case where the effect is skipped).

## 3. Same smoothness pass for other section animations
Review the other section entrances (cards, principles strip, steps, flow diagrams, device sequence) using the same measurement approach. Fix choppy ones, keep the existing visual style, and don't remove any effect.

## 4. Verify (don't claim anything you didn't measure)
- In the production build, record a heading reveal for the hero and for two other sections at 1440x900 and 390x844. Report dropped frames or long tasks before and after, and tell me what changed.
- Show me before/after of the first, middle, and last frames of one heading reveal so I can see the blur-to-sharp resolve is back.
- No layout shift, no console errors, reduced-motion works, lint and TypeScript checks pass.
- Report: root causes found, what fixed each, files changed, and anything you could not make smooth.


Also make sure the sessions should work like it was working in the demo, can add changes in code just done prediction through the options dont need to write anything. So make it easy for users.

## Headings
- **Restored from git:** each word goes from blurred (12px), faded and lowered to crisp, one by one. The frosted veil sweeps once per heading and leaves nothing behind. The accent word resolves last, the icon chips scale in with one glow, and each heading plays once at about 85% of the viewport. The hero uses the same effect on load.
- **Softer timing, as you specified:** 0.85s per word (1.05s for the accent), 70ms apart. Words are fully visible about halfway through while the blur keeps clearing, so they look like they're coming into focus.
- **Frames from a reveal slowed to 10% speed:** first, middle and last frames show blurred → focusing → crisp, with "why" last:
  - `C:\Users\NexGen\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Users-NexGen-Desktop-trace\ac502e61-757d-4998-a421-8b1428fcceb3\scratchpad\qa\heading-first-1440.png`
  - `C:\Users\NexGen\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Users-NexGen-Desktop-trace\ac502e61-757d-4998-a421-8b1428fcceb3\scratchpad\qa\heading-middle-1440.png`
  - `C:\Users\NexGen\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Users-NexGen-Desktop-trace\ac502e61-757d-4998-a421-8b1428fcceb3\scratchpad\qa\heading-last-1440.png`
  - The same three exist at 390px.
- **No "before" frames for the mask version:** I replaced that version without saving a copy, so I can't show its frames. The before numbers below come from the original blur code, rebuilt from git.

## What was choppy and what fixed it
Measured in production builds with Chrome tracing, 3 runs per case, after a warm-up:

| Case | Dropped frames before → after |
|---|---|
| Hero on load, 1440 | 24 → 4 |
| Learning-loop heading, 1440 | 23 → 11 |
| Learning-loop heading, 1440, 4× slower CPU | 16 → 8 |
| Features heading | 0–2, both |
| Everything at 390 | 0–8, both |

One caveat: Chrome's "partial frame" count swung from 0% to 50% between identical runs, so I relied on dropped frames only.

Causes found and fixes:
1. **One animation per property:** each word ran three separate animations (opacity, blur, rise). Now it's one native animation per word.
2. **Late layer promotion:** words got `will-change` in the same frame the animation started. Now they're promoted one frame earlier and released when done; the hero releases on finish.
3. **Off-screen loops:** two decorative loops (the device mesh drift and the streak drift) kept running when off-screen, forcing work every frame. They now pause off-screen.
4. **Flow diagram on phones:** it started each piece from JavaScript one at a time. It's now scheduled up front, and the straight connectors draw as compositor-scaled lines that look the same.
5. **Not causes:** fonts and the veil both measured as no real cost. I still hold the hero until fonts are ready, at no visible delay.
6. **A measurement trap:** the first page loads after a server start drop about 30 frames whatever the page does. That had inflated the early hero numbers; a warm-up removes it.

Also added: headings over 12 words blur as one block, and phones blur 6px while Safari and devices with 4 or fewer cores get 8px. The effect is never removed.

**Not fully fixed:** the flow diagram on phones still drops about 8 frames per reveal in my test browser, down from 9. That browser draws with the CPU, not a GPU, so real phones may do better.

Cards, principles strip, sticky steps and the device sequence measured 0–3 dropped frames, so I left them as they are.

## Lessons now work like the demo
- **Predict:** pick an option and lock it. The reason box is optional.
  - The backend still requires a reason, and I didn't change it. A blank reason is saved as the fixed text "No reason written.", which is never shown back.
  - **Gap for your colleague:** allow an empty `reasoning` in `PredictionCreate`.
- **Change:** you pick the field (`body`/`content`, or root/inside `metadata`) right inside the code line. One "Check my change" click saves the attempt and runs the real check; "Run verification" now only appears for retries.

## Checks
- Lint and TypeScript are clean.
- 72/72 unit tests pass.
- Playwright: 21 passed, 1 skipped (the keyboard test is desktop-only), against the real backend code on port 8010, since your server is on 8000.
- Homepage checks pass: reduced motion shows everything instantly, the no-JavaScript view is fully readable, layout shift is 0.009 at 1440 and 0 at 390, and there are no console errors.
- A full lesson completes without typing anything, at 1440 and 390.

**Changed files:** [motion-runtime.tsx](src/components/motion/motion-runtime.tsx), [tokens.ts](src/lib/motion/tokens.ts), [globals.css](src/app/globals.css), [layout.tsx](src/app/layout.tsx), [flow-diagram.tsx](src/components/marketing/flow-diagram.tsx), [device-sequence.tsx](src/components/marketing/device-sequence.tsx), [forms.tsx](src/components/learning/forms.tsx), and in [workspace.tsx](src/components/learning/workspace.tsx) one line that runs the check after saving. Plus test updates and CLAUDE.md's motion notes. `split-heading.tsx` is back to its git version.

# Trace repository status (as of 2 Nov 2026)

## 1. Project in one paragraph
Trace is a practice app for engineers who work with AI coding agents. Learners read real code, predict what it does, make one constrained change, and see the server verify the actual result (HTTP status, database rows). They revisit the idea later in new code. The product is not an AI tutor or code generator (CLAUDE.md). It is a working prototype run locally: Next.js frontend, FastAPI backend with SQLite, and Supabase sign-in. Two real lessons and two timed follow-ups work end to end. A large amount of backend infrastructure for AI-assisted lesson creation, tutoring, support and feedback exists, but no live AI provider is connected, and none of that new AI work has a public endpoint or any UI.

## 2. Who did what
- **Muhammad Sohaib** (18 commits): all frontend and design work.
  - Marketing homepage and motion system (`Motion stage 1–7`, Oct 30).
  - Lessons hub, auth/account redesign and lesson UI (`d8d6360`, Nov 1).
  - Merged PRs #1, #3, #4, #5 and #6.
- **Soorej S** (16 commits) and **SoorejS** (6): the same person under two author names. The SoorejS commits are GitHub merge commits.
  - All backend work (Modules 1–4; Phases 2.3, 2.4, 3.1, 3.2–3.4, 3.5A, 3.5B), the auth integration, the CI workflow, the session-logging tooling, and the architecture docs.
  - Also merged Sohaib's PR #4 into his branch and hardened the auth redirect (`cb4ad2e`, `1e7e0a0`).
- **Branches:**
  - Current local branch is `lessons-google-auth` at `52e8333`, the same commit as `origin/main`. Git reports it as "ahead 19" of `origin/lessons-google-auth`.
  - Local `main` (`a3ea743`) is 28 commits behind `origin/main`.
  - Local `feat/homepage-motion` sits at `d8d6360`.
  - Remote branches: `feat/trace-module-1-2`, `feat/trace-module-3-transfer-retention`, `feat/trace-module-4-account-auth`, `lessons-google-auth`, `main`.
- **PRs:** merge commits show PRs #1–#11 merged; the last is #11 (Phase 3.5A/B) on Nov 2. Open PRs are unknown: the GitHub CLI (`gh`) is not installed here.
- **Working tree:** one unstaged change, `.claude-logs/2026-10-29_14-04-11_ac502e61….md` (the session log). Nothing staged, nothing untracked.

## 3. What is built and working (code checked; tests not run by me)
**Backend** (`backend/app`: FastAPI modular monolith with SQLAlchemy and Alembic)
- **Learning loop:** anonymous guest cookie, sessions pinned to a lesson version, one prediction per session that can't be changed, numbered attempts, recorded hints, real verification and progress (`api/v1/learning.py`, `services/learning.py`, `services/verification.py`).
- **Verification:** sends a fixed request to private FastAPI apps (`verification/asgi_notes.py`, `asgi_tasks.py`), then reads the database back in a separate session.
- **Transfer lesson and follow-ups:** `domain/transfer.py`, `domain/retrieval.py`, `services/retrieval.py`.
- **Accounts:** Supabase tokens are checked against Supabase's published keys (`auth/jwt.py`), with roles and guest claiming (`services/accounts.py`).
- **Database:** migration head is `0007_tutor_reservations`. 25 tables, including `lessons`, `lesson_versions`, `learning_sessions`, `predictions`, `attempts`, `verification_runs`/`_checks`, `retrieval_checkpoints`/`_attempts`, `users`, `auth_subjects`, `account_learners`, `generated_candidates`, `candidate_lifecycle_events`, `tutor_interactions`, `hint_operations`, `runtime_notes`, `runtime_tasks`, `write_gate`.
- **Internal-only (no endpoint):** lesson contracts and blueprints (`domain/lesson_spec/`, `blueprints/candidates.py`), candidate validation and lifecycle, an engineering-evidence service, an evidence-feedback service, a daily-assignment service that always returns "blocked" (`services/daily_assignments.py`), and a support-knowledge base (`docs/support-drafts/`).

**Frontend** (`src/`: Next.js 16 App Router)
- **Marketing homepage** (`src/app/page.tsx`, `components/marketing/*`): sample Express demo labelled "Sample result", Motion and GSAP animations.
- **Lessons hub** (`/learn`): real progress, locking and follow-ups from the API.
- **Two lesson pages:** the workspace, a completion panel, and the change picked inside the code with one-click "Check my change" (`components/learning/*`).

**Auth/Accounts**
- Sign-in, sign-up, forgot and reset password, and the `/auth/callback` route, using Supabase with the PKCE sign-in flow (`components/auth/*`, `lib/supabase/*`, `src/proxy.ts`).
- `/account` dashboard with learning history and guest claim (`components/account/account-dashboard.tsx`).
- The Google button checks whether the provider is enabled first.
- Real Google sign-in and email delivery: the commit message for `d8d6360` says "google auth are now perfectly working". I did not verify that.

## 4. Lessons and learning flow today
- **Seeded lessons** (`backend/scripts/seed.py`, `services/catalog.py`), both version 1.0.0, Python/FastAPI:
  - `fastapi-payload-mismatch`, "Follow the note: from form to database". Field-name mismatch: `body` vs `content`.
  - `fastapi-nested-payload-transfer`, "The Nested Payload Problem". Wrong nesting level: `priority` must sit inside `metadata`.
- **Verification:**
  - The learner picks from a fixed set of mappings; no free-form code runs.
  - The server builds the request and calls a private route; Pydantic rejects or accepts it.
  - A fresh database session then reads the result: 422 and 0 rows, or 201 and the saved row.
  - An infrastructure error is never counted as a learner mistake.
- **Journey:**
  - Lesson 2 unlocks only after lesson 1 is completed on the server, and it requires running the flat request first.
  - Completing it schedules two follow-ups at +24h and +168h, each a 3-option question. Missed ones stay open, and the question appears only once due.

## 5. AI work status
- **Interfaces** (`backend/app/ai/`): `TutorProvider`, `LessonAssistant`, `QuestionGenerator`, `SupportAssistant`, `EvidenceFeedbackProvider`, `AITransport` and `BoundedAIClient`. "Bounded" adapters exist for each, plus fake/fixture versions and a `MockAITransport`.
- **Configuration** (`core/ai_settings.py`, `backend/ai.env.example`, names only): `TRACE_LLM_MODE` (allowed values: `disabled` or `mock` only), `TRACE_LLM_PROVIDER_ID`, `TRACE_LLM_GENERATION_MODEL_ID`, `TRACE_LLM_CONVERSATIONAL_MODEL_ID`, `TRACE_LLM_CREDENTIAL_ENV_VAR`, `TRACE_LLM_SPEND_LIMIT_MICROUSD`, `TRACE_LLM_MAX_COST_PER_ATTEMPT_MICROUSD`.
- **Live provider: none connected.** There's no OpenAI, Anthropic or Google SDK in `pyproject.toml`, no provider imports, and no provider URLs in `backend/app`. The mode setting can't select "live".
  - Claimed in `phase-3-1-provider-neutral.md`: no provider, model, credential or spending cap has been approved.
- **In the UI:** only hints, which come from `FakeTutorProvider` (`api/dependencies.py:62–64`) and are labelled "fixture guidance". No other AI feature has a screen.

## 6. API inventory (`backend/openapi.json`: 21 paths, 23 method+path operations)
| Area | Endpoints |
|---|---|
| Health | GET `/health/live`, GET `/health/ready` |
| Learners | POST `/learners/anonymous`, GET `/learners/me`, POST `/learners/me/revoke` |
| Journey | GET `/learners/me/journey` |
| Lessons | GET `/lessons/{lesson_id}` (optional `version` parameter) |
| Sessions | POST `/sessions`, GET `/sessions/{id}`, POST `/sessions/{id}/abandon`, GET/POST `/sessions/{id}/prediction`, GET/POST `/sessions/{id}/attempts`, POST `/sessions/{id}/hints`, GET `/sessions/{id}/progress` |
| Verification | POST `/sessions/{id}/attempts/{attempt_id}/verify`, GET `/sessions/{id}/verifications` |
| Retrievals | GET `/retrievals/{id}`, POST `/retrievals/{id}/attempts` |
| Account | GET `/account/me`, GET `/account/me/learner`, POST `/account/claim-anonymous` |

All paths are under `/api/v1`.

**No public endpoint yet:** lesson listing; lesson generation and the LessonAssistant; QuestionGenerator; the support assistant (RAG); evidence feedback; engineering evidence; candidate validation, lifecycle and publication; daily assignments; tutor provider selection; the creator/admin role API.

## 7. Frontend routes and their data source
| Route | Purpose | Data source |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | Marketing homepage with demo lesson | Static sample (Express example in `src/content/example.ts`) |
| `/learn` | Lessons hub, progress, follow-ups | Real API; labels from static `src/content/lessons.ts` |
| `/learn/fastapi-payload-mismatch` | Lesson 1 workspace | Real API |
| `/learn/fastapi-nested-payload-transfer` | Transfer lesson workspace | Real API |
| `/sign-in`, `/sign-up`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password` | Account auth | Supabase SDK |
| `/auth/callback` | Sign-in and email link callback | Supabase SDK on the server |
| `/account` | History, claim, details | Real API plus Supabase user data |

## 8. Tests, CI, and quality
- **Backend:** 59 test files (34 unit, 22 integration, 3 contract). Pytest is configured to fail below 85% coverage.
  - Claimed in `evidence-grounded-feedback-v1.md`: 2,635 passed, 97%+ coverage.
- **Frontend:** 7 Vitest files (`tests/components`) and 6 Playwright specs (`tests/e2e`, including a new `legacy-version.spec.ts`).
- **CI** (`.github/workflows/quality.yml`):
  - Backend: ruff, ruff format, mypy, pytest, Alembic upgrade/check, and an OpenAPI drift check.
  - Frontend: lint, typecheck, Vitest, API-types drift check, build, Playwright.
  - Remote CI results are unknown: no `gh`.
- **Run by me:** tests not run by me for this summary.
  - Earlier in this session I ran lint, typecheck, Vitest (72 passed), Playwright (21 passed, 1 skipped) and backend pytest (211 passed) on an older tree. Those results predate Soorej's Phase 2.3–3.5 commits.

## 9. Known gaps, risks, and TODOs
- No `TODO`/`FIXME` in `src` or `backend/app`. The risks are structural.
- Only 2 lessons exist. There's no lesson list endpoint, so lesson labels are hard-coded in the frontend (`src/content/lessons.ts`).
- The prediction API requires `reasoning` of at least one character. The frontend sends the fixed text "No reason written." when the box is left blank.
- Hints are fixed fixture text from `FakeTutorProvider`. Hints wired to a real provider would need `TutorProvider` plus a live transport, and neither is approved.
- Daily assignments are intentionally blocked: there's no learner timezone and no assignment table (`daily-assignment-readiness.md`).
- Personalization has no approved interests or skill policy (`personalization-readiness.md`).

My backend teammate delivered two files that are the authoritative contract: `frontend-api-integration.md` and an OpenAPI export (`openapi.json`, expected at `docs/api/openapi.json`). Find them in the repo and folder (search by name if they are elsewhere), read BOTH completely before touching code, and treat them as the source of truth. Don't rebuild what already works. The goal is to verify and fix the existing frontend against this contract.

## Scope and rules
- Frontend only. Don't change backend code, migrations, `backend/openapi.json`, or env files. If something needs a backend change, list it for my teammate and stop there.
- Work on the real Notes workflow (`fastapi-payload-mismatch`) and the real Tasks transfer workflow (`fastapi-nested-payload-transfer`), the journey/hub, retrievals (follow-ups), and the guest-versus-account identity flow.
- DO NOT build or call anything the document lists as INTERNAL ONLY or NOT IMPLEMENTED: live tutoring, support assistant, evidence feedback, generation, daily assignments, quotas, mastery, a lessons list endpoint, creator APIs. Never infer URLs from service or schema names. Hints stay fixture-labeled ("fixture guidance", `provider: "fake"`), not AI.
- Reuse the existing API client (`src/lib/api/client.ts`) and generated types (`src/lib/api/schema.d.ts`). No duplicate clients or hand-copied interfaces. If the types are stale versus `docs/api/openapi.json`, regenerate with the existing `npm run api:types` and report the diff.
- Never print, log, or commit tokens, cookies, env values, or request payloads containing private content.
- Don't run git push, don't open a PR, and don't commit unless I ask. Leave the unstaged `.claude-logs/*` file alone. Work on the current branch if it's a feature branch, otherwise create `feat/frontend-real-api-integration` from the latest `origin/main` and tell me.
- Keep the approved visual system, animations, and accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA, keyboard, aria-live for async results, reduced motion, 320 to 1920 responsive).

## Phase 0: Read and compare (no code changes yet)
1. Read both files in full. Then read the existing frontend integration: `src/lib/api/*`, `src/lib/supabase/*`, `src/proxy.ts`, `src/app/learn/**`, `src/components/learning/**`, `src/components/account/**`, `src/components/auth/**`, `src/content/lessons.ts`, and `tests/**`.
2. Produce a SHORT conformance table (one row per item below) with: Status (OK / Gap / Wrong / Unknown), file and line evidence, and the fix needed. Then continue directly into Phase 1. Don't wait for me unless something blocks.

Conformance checklist (verify each against the document, don't assume):
A. Base URL and hostnames: the client uses an API origin WITHOUT `/api/v1` and adds the prefix itself; `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL` override works; frontend and API use the same hostname locally (localhost with localhost, 127.0.0.1 with 127.0.0.1).
B. Every request uses `credentials: "include"`, `cache: "no-store"`, a timeout (about 35s), the `X-Trace-Client: learning-web` header on POSTs, JSON content type, and the `Authorization: Bearer <Supabase access token>` header only when signed in (access token, not ID or refresh token).
C. Idempotency: a UUID `Idempotency-Key` is generated ONCE per logical user action and kept with the pending request. It is reused with the same body on retry or transport uncertainty. A deliberate new correction or new verification retry gets a new key. Operations needing a Key: create session, prediction, attempt, hint, verify, retrieval answer, claim.
D. 204 handling: the guest revoke call returns 204 with no body, and the client must not call `.json()` on it.
E. Error handling by `error.code` and status per the document's table (401, 403, 404, 409, 422, 429, 503, 413, 504, 500, 405): preserve drafts and keys, show the `request_id` in a collapsed support detail, never show raw submitted values, never treat 500/503/504 as a learner mistake, and don't retry-loop on 403. For 504 or uncertain writes, read saved progress first and replay the SAME key.
F. Identity flows: guest bootstrap `POST /learners/anonymous` with `{}`; call it on return to renew; 401 on protected reads means "needs identity", not "empty history"; an invalid Bearer must NOT silently fall back to the guest cookie (after 401, refresh through the Supabase SDK).
G. THE SIGNED-IN ORDER (critical): after sign-in, call `GET /account/me` (Bearer plus cookie) BEFORE any journey/session/learner call. If `anonymous_history_available` is true and `learner_id` is null, show the explicit claim choice first (`POST /account/claim-anonymous`, body `{}`, one stable key, cookie plus Bearer). Only if the user declines, continue to an ordinary learner. After a successful claim, refresh account and journey. Never auto-claim or merge. Check that no component fires `/learners/me/journey` or similar eagerly for a signed-in user before this decision (navbar, hub, `/account`, `/learn`). Also handle 409 (incompatible history) and 429 (30 per minute inspect/claim limits, with no Retry-After header).
H. Notes flow, in order: read journey, resume a session from `phases[].sessions` or explicitly create one, render pinned `progress.lesson` (not the latest catalog), submit the immutable prediction once (`selected_answer` is rejected | saved | server_error, and `reasoning` is REQUIRED 1 to 2000 characters), save a bounded attempt (`change_type: rename_payload_field`, `field_name: body | content`, optional `explanation`), then verify the saved attempt. Check the placeholder reasoning behavior ("No reason written.") and make the UI text honest about it.
I. Tasks flow: Notes completion unlocks Tasks (409 on prerequisite is shown as "complete Notes first", not a crash); first submit `nest_payload_field` with `field_name: flat` and verify it (it should fail with real evidence); only then offer `nested`; a passed run completes Tasks and schedules two checkpoints at +24h and +168h.
J. Verification results: `RunResponse.status` of `failed` means the learner mapping failed (HTTP 200); `error` is INFRASTRUCTURE and not a learner failure, with the session staying `verification_pending` and a deliberate "Check again" using a NEW key after reading progress; `pending` and 409 reconcile via progress, never invent a polling or job endpoint. Checks are strings (`expected`, `observed`, `source`), rendered as plain text or code, never HTML. Normal runs have five checks (`http_status`, `api_response`, `validation`, `row_count`, `stored_values`), but an error run may show an `execution` check instead, so never assume exactly five.
K. Hints: `POST /sessions/{id}/hints` with an optional `attempt_id`; max 3 request slots per session; show slots from `summary.hint_request_count` and `hints_delivered`, not from `hints.length`; `status: "error"` returns 201 with `text: null`, consumes a slot, and renders as "unavailable"; label everything "fixture guidance". Prediction must exist first.
L. Progress as the authority: after EVERY mutation, refresh `GET /sessions/{id}/progress`, and the journey where relevant. Completion UI appears ONLY from server state `completed`. Never derive correctness, completion, or `prediction_correct` on the client. `summary` flags (correct_after_revision, hint_assisted_correction) are shown as factual statements, never as scores or mastery.
M. Retrievals: use `checkpoint.status` and the backend `server_time`, never the browser clock, to enable answering. `upcoming` has `question: null`. Answer with `selection` a | b | c plus optional reasoning and a stable key. Incorrect answers persist with feedback and the checkpoint stays due. Correct completes it. Exact replay after completion is fine, new answers are not (409).
N. Abandon: an explicit confirm button, `POST /sessions/{id}/abandon` with `{}`; a completed session can't be abandoned (409).
O. Other: `GET /lessons/{lesson_id}` with the optional `version` query; ids are slugs, not `version_id`; there's no lessons collection endpoint, so discovery is via journey phases and the static registry; all UUID path values are `encodeURIComponent`-safe; no caching of owned responses.

## Phase 1: Fix gaps (smallest correct change each, in priority order)
1. Identity and ordering (items F, G): this one is the highest risk.
2. Idempotency and retry correctness (C, E).
3. Verification and hint semantics (J, K, L).
4. Notes and Tasks sequencing and states (H, I, N, O).
5. Retrievals (M).
6. Everything else from the table.
Write each fix with the existing component structure. Add a short in-code comment where a rule comes from the contract document (for example "backend: error run is infrastructure, not a learner failure"), and don't leave TODO noise.

For every screen touched, confirm these states exist and look right: loading, success, empty (for example no sessions yet, `null` prediction, `[]` attempts), recoverable error with retry and request ID detail, and "mutation pending" with duplicate submission disabled and drafts preserved.

## Phase 2: Tests

Frontend-complete build for every Trace feature whose API does not exist yet, plus a requirements document for my backend teammate. Read first, build second.

## Context
- `frontend-api-integration.md` (find it in the repo, probably root or docs/) and `openapi.json` are the authoritative contract. The real API covers the Notes and Tasks workflows, hub, follow-ups, and the guest/account flow, and they are already integrated. Everything that document marks INTERNAL ONLY or NOT IMPLEMENTED has NO public endpoint: live tutor Q&A or explanations, "why did this fail" feedback, the support assistant (RAG), evidence feedback, generated exercises, daily assignments, quotas, interests, mastery, a lessons list.
- The only AI-like thing that is real is `POST /sessions/{id}/hints`, which returns fixed fixture text (`provider: "fake"`).
- My teammate owns the backend, AI services, provider integration, and public API contracts. I own the whole frontend. The RAG and LLM live in the BACKEND, not in the frontend.

## Hard rules
- Frontend only. Don't change backend code, migrations, `openapi.json`, or any env file. Don't add Next.js server routes, route handlers, or server actions that call any LLM, search index, or provider, and don't add any provider SDK. No LLM keys or provider credentials anywhere in the frontend, including `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`.
- Never invent real endpoints or call URLs that are not in `openapi.json`. Real clients for missing features throw a clear "endpoint not available yet" error. Never present a mock result as AI output, real verification, or real learner history. Never write mock results into real progress.
- AI explains and guides. Deterministic backend verification decides correctness and completion. The UI must never say the tutor decided that a learner is correct.
- Python/FastAPI learning scope only. No other languages, no Creator Mode.
- Keep the approved visual system, motion tokens, accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA, keyboard, aria-live for async responses, reduced motion, 320 to 1920 responsive). Original SVG/CSS art only, no stock photos.
- Don't print or commit secrets. Don't run git push, don't open a PR, don't commit unless I ask. Leave `.claude-logs/*` alone. Work on a feature branch from the latest `origin/main` (`feat/frontend-ai-surfaces`), unless the current branch is already a clean feature branch. Tell me which.

## Phase 0: Read and report (no code yet)
1. Read `frontend-api-integration.md` completely, `openapi.json`, CLAUDE.md, `docs/` (especially anything on support drafts, daily-assignment and personalization readiness, phase 3.x), `src/lib/api/*`, the lesson, hub, account and marketing components, `src/content/*`, and the tests.
2. Update CLAUDE.md: AI features explain, guide, and support but never decide correctness; mocks are always labeled; credentials never touch the frontend; the RAG and LLM are backend-owned. Show me the diff.
3. Send a SHORT report (under 30 lines): the list of features with no public endpoint (from the document), what already exists in the frontend for each, and your plan. Then continue without waiting.

## Phase 1: Adapter layer with clearly identified mocks (`src/lib/ai/`)
One module, no scattered fake data:
- `contracts.ts`: hand-written PROPOSED types for every missing capability (header comment: "PROPOSED. Not a backend contract. Replace with generated types when published."). Cover: tutor ask (lesson-scoped), concept explanation, why-did-this-fail (references a real verification run id), support ask (answer, sources[{id,title,link,snippet}], optional escalation, unavailable state), interests, today's three exercises, exercise completion, generated exercise retrieval.
- `client.ts`: typed `TutorClient`, `SupportClient`, `DailyLearningClient` and a factory selected by `NEXT_PUBLIC_AI_MODE=mock|real` (default `mock`; the only AI-related frontend variable). `real` implementations call our backend through the EXISTING API client and throw "endpoint not available yet" until the endpoint exists. The hints button uses the real `POST /sessions/{id}/hints` in both modes (label it "Fixture guidance", slots from `summary.hint_request_count` and `hints_delivered`).
- `mock/`: scripted mocks with realistic latency, optional streaming, and test switches for error, rate-limited, empty, unavailable, and unauthenticated states. Content must be correct and grounded in the REAL Notes lesson (`body` vs `content`; extra fields forbidden; validation failure means 422 and no row) and Tasks lesson (`priority` must sit inside `metadata`). Mock hints ladder from nudge to bigger clue to a deliberate "Show worked explanation" last step, and never reveal the full solution early.
- Labeling: whenever a mock drives a feature, show a persistent "Demo responses" indicator on it, with an accessible tooltip ("Scripted while the AI backend is being connected"). Mock output never changes real hint counts, verification, or journey.

## Phase 2: Customer support assistant on EVERY page
- A floating support launcher fixed at the bottom-right of every route (marketing, auth, hub, lessons, account, and the new pages), with safe-area padding on mobile. On lesson pages, position it so it never overlaps the tutor panel, the mobile tutor drawer, the submit controls, or the completion panel (for example, lift it above the mobile action bar, or shift it left of the docked tutor panel). Hide it only on the sign-in callback processing screen.
- Distinct identity from the tutor: name ("Trace Help"), icon, accent, and tone. Opens a chat panel (desktop popover about 380px, mobile bottom sheet) with focus trap, Escape to close, focus restoration, and `aria-live` for new answers.
- Content: welcome message, starter questions (How do I sign up? I can't sign in with Google, Where is my progress, How do follow-ups work, What is a fixture hint), message input with a length limit and counter, a "Never share passwords or secret keys" notice, new-chat button, in-memory conversation only (not persisted), and a short privacy note.
- Answers show source references (title and internal link), and a "Talk to a human" action ONLY when a response includes escalation, or via `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL` (mailto) if set; hide otherwise. No invented ticketing system.
- States: loading, streaming, empty, error with retry, rate-limited, unavailable (the document says support is unavailable by default, so this must look intentional and helpful: show the starter FAQ and the human option), and the mock-mode "Demo responses" label.
- Mock RAG-lite for the demo: a typed local help set in `src/content/support/` (id, title, body, route link, keywords) written from the real product behavior (accounts, guest history claim, lessons, follow-up timing, fixture hints, why verification errors are not your mistake, cookies and same-hostname local setup). If `docs/support-drafts/` exists, base the articles on it faithfully (read-only). The mock does simple keyword scoring over this set and returns the best 1 to 3 articles as sources with a templated answer. It must be obviously a demo and must never fabricate sources that aren't in the local set. Questions it cannot match get an "I'm not sure" answer plus the human option.

## Phase 3: AI tutor surface in the Notes and Tasks lessons
- Tutor panel on lesson pages (docked on desktop, bottom drawer on mobile) receiving lesson id, current step, and the real evidence already loaded (`progress` and verification checks). Actions: "Explain this concept" (mock), "Give me a hint" (REAL fixture hint endpoint, ladder of 3 slots, labeled fixture), "Why did this fail?" (appears only after a real failed verification, uses the real run's checks as context, mock explanation), and a free-text question box (mock).
- AI content is visibly separate from deterministic evidence and uses the label "AI explanation (demo)". The tutor never says a learner is correct or complete. The full worked explanation is a deliberate last step with a confirm.
- Preserve prediction lock, attempts, verification, and hint accounting. Mock output never touches them.
- States: loading, streaming, error, rate-limited, unavailable, guest-not-allowed, empty. Privacy note under the input.

## Phase 4: Daily dashboard `/today` and interests (design preview)
- Three-exercise path (theory, interest-based coding exercise, transfer/debugging) with sequential unlocking, factual statuses, animated trace line, and a factual completion state, no scores.
- Interest picker (chips, keyboard accessible, 1 to 5, skip option), editable from `/today` and `/account`. Mock persistence in `localStorage`, labeled "demo-only".
- The document says daily assignment, quotas, and timezone policy are NOT implemented, so label the page "Design preview: daily exercises are not connected to the backend yet". Exercises that map to the real Notes or Tasks lessons link to the real lesson routes and use real status from the journey. Mock-only exercises are labeled "Demo exercise, backend verification not connected" and never mark real progress.
- Link `/today` from the navbar and the hub header.

## Phase 5: Backend requirements for my teammate (write these documents)
Create `docs/frontend-api-requests.md` with every API and decision the frontend needs. This is the deliverable I send to my teammate. Make it specific, grouped, and prioritized (P0, P1, P2). For EACH proposed endpoint give: method and path suggestion, auth rules (guest cookie or account Bearer), request schema, response schema (with an example), error codes using the existing `ErrorResponse` envelope, idempotency key requirement, rate limits and `Retry-After` behavior, streaming or not (and the stream format if yes), no-store header, and what the frontend does when it is unavailable.
Cover at least:
1. Tutor ask, concept explanation, and why-did-this-fail (should reference a stored `verification run id`, so the backend uses stored evidence, never learner-supplied evidence). Hint-level ownership (server decides, client displays). Guest access policy. Solution-leak policy.
2. Support assistant: `POST` ask returning `answer`, `sources[]`, `escalation` (nullable), `unavailable` state; conversation id or stateless; whether history is stored; the approved help corpus and who owns it; the escalation destination (email, form, or ticket system); logging and retention policy for support questions; PII handling.
3. Daily assignment and interests: endpoints for the interest taxonomy, set and get learner interests, today's three exercises, exercise status and completion; the meaning of "daily" (calendar day in which timezone, learner timezone field, reset time); quota enforcement and its error code; how generated interest-based exercises are verified.
4. Generated exercise retrieval and its verification contract.
5. A lessons list endpoint with labels (concept, bug type, duration), so the hard-coded registry can go.
6. The four open questions from the last integration report: optional prediction reasoning, no-store on every error, the client 35s timeout versus server 30s and 10s limits, and a lesson list.
7. A section "What the RAG backend needs" (a recommendation for my teammate, not an implementation): the corpus format (markdown articles with id, title, tags, last-reviewed date, route link), chunking, SQLite FTS5 first and embeddings later, retrieval limits, answer-only-from-sources rule, citation format, "I'm not sure" threshold, prompt-injection defenses for retrieved text and learner text, no learner-private data in retrieval, cost and rate controls, evaluation set of 30 sample support questions with expected sources (generate the question list from the mock help set), and an update process for the corpus.
8. A short "Frontend adapter swap checklist": the exact steps and files to change when real endpoints arrive (flip `NEXT_PUBLIC_AI_MODE`, add generated types via `npm run api:types`, replace each `real` client body, delete the mock label per feature, update tests).

## Phase 6: Tests
- Vitest: adapter factory (mock versus real, real throws "not available yet"), support launcher on every route (including lessons without overlapping controls at 320, 390, 768, 1280), support chat states (loading, streaming, sources, escalation present or absent, unmatched question gives "I'm not sure", rate-limited, unavailable), tutor panel states and hint ladder, "Why did this fail?" only after a real failed run, mock label always visible in mock mode, daily path unlocking and interest limits, no mock output ever written to real progress or hint counts.
- Playwright (desktop and Pixel 7) with `NEXT_PUBLIC_AI_MODE=mock` and the repo's test backend (temporary migrated and seeded database, not my real database or any hosted service): support launcher visible on homepage, sign-in, hub, a lesson, and account; opens, asks, sees sources, closes with focus restored; real fixture hint plus mock tutor on a lesson; `/today` flow; keyboard-only path; no page errors. Tests never call a real provider.
- Keep all existing tests passing and don't weaken assertions.

## Phase 7: Verify (don't claim what you didn't run)
- Build and run a production build. Capture screenshots at 1440x900, 1024, 768, 390x844, and 320: the launcher on five different pages, the open support chat with sources, the unavailable state, a lesson with the tutor panel and the launcher both present, the mobile drawer, and `/today`. Inspect them and fix overlap, wrapping, overflow, and contrast.
- Run lint, typecheck, Vitest, `npm run api:types` drift check, and Playwright. Report each command with its real result and what you could not run.

**Files created**

**Files changed:** `layout.tsx` (Help launcher on every page), `workspace.tsx` (tutor panel), `globals.css`, nav links (adds "Today"; inline nav now starts at 1024px, menu below that), `/learn` header (link to `/today`), `/account` (interest picker), `account-link.tsx` (no wrapping at 320px).

**Real vs demo**
- **Real:**
  - **Lessons:** both lessons, verification, the journey and follow-ups.

- **Demo:** Trace Help answers, all tutor text, interests, and `/today` steps 1 and 2.

  - **"Demo responses":** in the Trace Help header and the tutor header, with the tooltip "Scripted while the AI backend is being connected".
  - **Tutor replies:** "AI explanation (demo)" on each one.
  - **`/today`:** "Demo-only" on interests; "Demo exercise, backend verification not connected" on steps 1–2; a "Design preview: daily exercises are not connected to the backend yet" banner.
- **Isolation:** tests confirm demo output never calls a write endpoint or changes progress.

**`docs/frontend-api-requests.md` priorities**
- **P0:** optional prediction reasoning, `no-store` on every error, the 35s vs 30s/10s timeouts, `GET /lessons`.
- **P1:** tutor (explain, ask, why-failed by stored run id, worked explanation); support assistant (sources and escalation).
- **P2:** interests, today's exercises and the meaning of "daily", generated exercises.
- **Also included:** the RAG recommendations and the frontend swap checklist.

- Lint clean; typecheck passed; Vitest 119/119; production build compiled.
- `api:types`: no content drift.
- Playwright: 39 passed, 3 skipped (desktop-only tests), desktop and Pixel 7, mock mode, on the repo's test backend with a temporary database on port 8010.

2. **Tutor and guests:** can guests use the tutor? Should tutor exchanges and worked-explanation views be stored as learning events?

## Product decisions from my backend teammate (follow exactly)
1. PRIMARY: a contextual Lesson Tutor panel integrated into every lesson. It understands the current lesson and step. The learner can ask questions about the lesson, request concept explanations, get progressive hints, ask why their request or code failed, and understand concepts WITHOUT simply receiving the answer. Preserve the tutor panel experience in my screenshot (header with "Demo responses" badge, "Explain this concept", "Give me a hint (n of 3 used)", fixture guidance card, chat bubbles, "AI EXPLANATION (DEMO)" card, "Ask about this lesson" box with counter and privacy line).

My teammate will deliver API contracts for both capabilities incrementally.

Hints keep using the real fixture endpoint `POST /sessions/{id}/hints` (label "Fixture guidance"). Other tutor actions stay behind the existing `src/lib/ai/` adapter with clearly labeled mocks (`NEXT_PUBLIC_AI_MODE`). Create that adapter if it doesn't exist yet. Reuse it if it does. Don't duplicate API clients. No LLM keys in the frontend.

## Task 1: Fix the lesson page layout (both lessons)
Problems in the screenshot: code windows need horizontal scrolling and cut off lines (for example `client.post("/tasks", json=pay`), the same payload appears twice (left code window and the "run the original request" card), and the three-column layout makes every column too narrow.
- Redesign the layout so all lesson code is fully visible without horizontal scrolling at 1024px and wider, and on mobile. Do NOT shrink text below 13px to achieve this.
  - Desktop (1280 and up): two main areas plus the tutor: a left column (about 52% of the content width) with lesson context and the code windows, a right-center column (about 48%) with the step cards, and the tutor panel as a sticky, independently scrolling right-hand panel (about 340 to 380px) with a collapse button that gives the content more room. At 1024 to 1279, make the tutor a collapsible docked panel or a right-edge drawer so content keeps at least about 560px.
  - Tablet and mobile: single column. The tutor opens as a bottom drawer (with a visible launcher that doesn't collide with the support launcher at the bottom-right: if both exist, stack them or place the tutor launcher in the lesson action bar).
- Code windows: keep the MacBook-style frame, line numbers, and copy button. Make long lines soft-wrap with a hanging indent that preserves indentation (`white-space: pre-wrap` plus per-line wrapping that keeps the line number gutter aligned) or restructure the window so wrapping is readable. Horizontal scrollbars appear only as a last resort for unusually long tokens, never for normal lesson code. Check against the actual lesson code strings from the API for both lessons.
- Remove the duplication: show each code block once. The step card that previously repeated the payload should reference the single code window (for example by highlighting the relevant lines) or BE the editable one (see Task 2).
- Keep the visual system, motion tokens, and the blur-to-sharp heading reveal. Keep all existing states: prediction lock, "You didn't write a reason" system note, attempts, verification evidence, hint slots, completion panel, abandon.

## Task 2: Make the "change" step an in-code edit (like the homepage interactive demo)
Find the homepage interactive demo component (look under `src/components/marketing/` and `src/content/`) and reuse its interaction pattern and styling for the real lessons: the learner edits the change INSIDE the code, not by choosing from a separate option list.
- Constraint from the backend (do not violate): attempts are bounded mappings. Notes accepts `change_type: rename_payload_field` with `field_name: body | content`. Tasks accepts `change_type: nest_payload_field` with `field_name: flat | nested`. Anything else is rejected. So the in-code control must produce exactly these values. No free-form code input, no arbitrary text sent to the backend, and no claim that arbitrary code runs.
- Notes: in the code window, the dictionary key token (`body`) becomes an inline editable chip (a button styled like a code token with a caret). Clicking it opens a small popover or inline segmented control with the allowed values (`body`, `content`), and the code line updates live. The changed line is highlighted as a mini diff (old line struck through or tinted, new line highlighted). Don't show the option list separately.
- Tasks: the line `"priority": task.priority,` carries an inline control ("Nest this field") that rewrites the payload in place into the nested shape (`"metadata": { "priority": task.priority }`), with a toggle to undo back to flat. The code reflows with a short transition, and the diff highlight shows the changes. The sequencing from the backend contract stays: the learner must first run the original FLAT request (mandatory failure evidence) before the nested edit unlocks.
- Under the code, one primary action: "Check my change". It performs the existing real flow (save the attempt with a fresh idempotency key, then verify, reusing keys on uncertain retries exactly as the existing client does) and displays the real evidence. Keep an optional "What changed and why?" textarea (maps to `explanation`, max 2000 chars). Don't change the request or verification logic, only the UI that feeds it.
- A short honest note near the control: "You edit this line inside the code. Trace checks a fixed set of changes against the real server." Show a "Reset" button that restores the original line.
- Accessibility: the chip is a real button with an accessible name ("Change field name, currently body"), a keyboard-operable popover (arrow keys, Enter, Escape), and focus restored on close. Respect reduced motion. Minimum 44px tap target on mobile.
- Verification behaves exactly as before: failed means the mapping failed (edit again), `error` is infrastructure ("Check again"), completion appears only from server `completed`.

## Task 3: Tutor panel refinement (preserve the mockup's design)
- Keep the look and the structure in the screenshot. Add: a context line at the top that shows what the tutor sees ("Lesson: The Nested Payload Problem. Step: Run the original request"), updating as the learner moves through steps. Send lesson id, step, and the real evidence already on the page as context to the adapter (never anything private beyond that).
- Actions: "Explain this concept" (mock), "Give me a hint" (REAL fixture endpoint, three slots shown from `summary.hint_request_count` and `hints_delivered`), "Why did this fail?" (appears only after a real failed verification and receives that run's checks as context; mock explanation), and the free-text question box (mock, length limit and counter).
- Behaviors: the final worked explanation is a deliberate last step with a confirm. The tutor never says a learner is right or finished. AI output uses the "AI explanation (demo)" label and is visually distinct from the verification evidence. Chat history is kept in memory for the page session. Include loading, streaming (if the adapter supports it), error with retry, rate-limited, unavailable, guest-not-allowed, and empty states, `aria-live` for new answers, focus management for the drawer, and the privacy line.
- The mock content must be correct and grounded in the REAL lessons (Notes: key name mismatch and forbidden extra fields lead to 422 and no row; Tasks: `priority` must be inside `metadata`), and must follow the hint ladder and never reveal the full solution early.

## Task 4: Lesson library prepared for future AI-generated lessons
In the hub (`/learn`), restructure into a lesson library:
- Section "Trace lessons": the two real lessons (existing cards, status and locking from the API).
- Section "Your lessons": an honest empty state: "Custom lessons you create will appear here." No fake lessons.
- A "Create a custom lesson" card with a designed request form UI: topic (text, with example placeholder "I want to learn FastAPI authentication from beginner to intermediate with practical exercises"), level selector (beginner to intermediate), a preference for practical exercises, and a clear preview of what a generated lesson will contain (explanations, coding challenges, verification criteria) shown as a static illustration labeled "Preview of the format". The submit button is DISABLED with the visible message "Coming soon: custom lessons are being built" and no fake loading, no fake output, and no mock generated lesson. Don't call any endpoint.
- Architecture only: define a small `LessonSource` abstraction (catalog from the existing static registry plus real journey data now; generated source is a stub that reports "unavailable") so a future real source slots in. Add PROPOSED types for lesson-creation requests and generated lesson structure in `src/lib/ai/contracts.ts` with the "PROPOSED, not a backend contract" header. Nothing calls them.
- Make sure Python/FastAPI is the only scope shown in the form examples.

## Task 5: Docs for my teammate
Create or update `docs/frontend-api-requests.md` with proposed contracts for BOTH capabilities (and keep any existing content):
1. Contextual tutor: ask, explain concept, why-did-this-fail (should accept a stored verification run id so the backend uses stored evidence, never learner-supplied evidence), hint-level ownership, guest access, solution-leak policy, streaming or not, rate limits, error codes, and no-store behavior.

AI lesson creation: request creation (topic, level, practical-exercise preference), asynchronous status and retrieval (queued, generating, ready, failed, needs-review), the structure of a generated lesson (explanation sections, coding challenges, verification criteria), how generated coding challenges get VERIFIED (the current verification only supports the two hand-built runtimes), review and approval, quotas and cost caps, safety, list endpoint for a learner's custom lessons, and how generated lessons appear in the journey.
