Challenge 2 has no code because the lesson was generated as a plain multiple-choice question; its code field is empty. It can't work like the demo with frontend changes alone. The demo checks your line against an expected fix written into the page, and AI-created lessons contain no expected fix and no editable line. So I made the frontend honest about it instead of adding code the lesson doesn't have:

- **Challenges without code** (like challenge 2): step 3 is now **"Choose"**. You pick the answer to check, with no "Try a change in the code" button and no "Your code isn't run" note.
- **Challenges with code:** unchanged. "Try a change in the code" lets you click a line and type your version, and Enter checks it, as in the demo. Your line is shown as "Your change · Not run". The answer you choose is what's checked against the answer key.
- **Explain challenges without code:** step 3 is "Revise" (you refine your explanation).

**Tutor:** it stays as built. It explains from the lesson's own content, gives the lesson's hints, and answers "Why didn't it match?" without giving away the answer. AI answers to typed questions need a backend endpoint for custom lessons, so they aren't possible from the frontend.

**What would make it fully demo-like:** a backend change, recorded in `docs/backend-issue-report.md` for your teammate. Every challenge would need code, one editable line and its expected fix, plus a tutor endpoint for custom lessons. Once those fields exist, the frontend can use them directly.

**Checks:** typecheck and lint are clean, and Vitest passes 232 of 232, including a new test for the no-code challenge. I didn't rerun Playwright for this change; it only changes labels.

**Files changed:** `src/components/learning/custom/challenge-loop.tsx` and `tests/components/custom-lessons.test.tsx`. The commit commands from my last message still apply.

Task: build two connected frontend features for Trace with premium UI/UX and animation: (A) a redesigned `/account` page with default avatars and custom-lesson data, and (B) the V1 global leaderboard at `/leaderboard`. Read CLAUDE.md, `frontend-api-integration.md` and newer handoff docs, `backend/openapi.json`, `src/lib/api/*`, `src/lib/ai/*` (adapter pattern), the navbar and user menu, the account page and `src/components/account/*`, the lessons library and My lessons pages, the lesson completion panel, and the existing tests before coding.

HARD RULES
- FRONTEND ONLY. Do NOT edit `backend/`, migrations, `backend/openapi.json`, env files, or the launcher. Use only endpoints that exist in `openapi.json`. Whatever the backend lacks gets an honest unavailable state and an entry in `docs/frontend-api-requests.md` plus the final report. No invented endpoints, numbers, mastery scores, XP, streaks, points, or fake progress.
- Before editing: `git status`, create a backup branch (`git branch backup-before-account-leaderboard`), work on `feat/account-leaderboard`. Don't commit, don't push, leave `.claude-logs/*` alone. Never print secrets, env values, tokens, emails, or learner content.
- Keep the approved design system, tokens, the shared `SplitHeading` blur-to-sharp heading reveal, motion tokens, WCAG 2.2 AA, keyboard use, aria-live, reduced motion, responsive 320 to 1920. Original SVG art only (no stock photos, no photos of real people).
- AI-created (custom) lessons are answer-key or practice-only. Never call them "verified by the server" and never count them toward the leaderboard.
- The backend is the only source of truth for leaderboard counts, ranks, ties, and order. The frontend never computes, sorts, or sends a score or count.

PART 0: SHARED AVATAR (build first, used everywhere)
- Create an original SVG avatar set (12 to 16 friendly, abstract, gender-neutral characters or shapes on warm palette backgrounds; no real faces, no brand characters) and one `Avatar` component used in the navbar menu, the account header, leaderboard rows, and lesson completion panels.
- Default: a deterministic avatar derived from a stable id. Never an initial letter. Skeleton avatar while loading. Alt text "Avatar" only (no email or id).
- Picker on `/account`: accessible dialog (focus trap, Escape, arrow keys, `aria-pressed`, preview). Persist `avatar_id` in the signed-in user's metadata through the EXISTING Supabase SDK integration (`updateUser`), validate against the allowlist on read and write (ignore anything else), localStorage fallback if saving fails, with saving, success, and error states. Never load third-party provider photos automatically.

PART A: ACCOUNT PAGE (`/account`)
1. Root cause first: why custom lessons don't show. Compare how `/account` loads data with how My lessons loads custom lessons (endpoint, hook, query keys, auth mode). Report the cause and fix it with the existing API client and generated types (`npm run api:types`; restore line endings if that is the only diff). Creating, progressing, completing, or deleting a custom lesson must update both My lessons and `/account` (shared query keys and invalidation).
2. Header card: avatar with "Change avatar", display name (provider profile name, falling back to "Trace learner", never the email local part), confirmed email (owner only), "Member since", one primary action ("Continue learning" or "Start a lesson").
3. Stats strip, REAL data only: Trace lessons completed (X of Y), custom lessons created, custom lessons completed, total attempts, hints used. Hide any stat the API doesn't provide. A compact "Your rank" card appears only when the leaderboard returns real data for the user.
4. Accessible, URL-synced tabs: Overview (stats, "Continue where you left off" only if something is in progress, 3 recent activities), Learning history, Follow-ups, Settings.
   - Learning history: "Trace lessons" (the existing factual per-lesson timeline: prediction, attempts, verified results, hints, completion times) and "My lessons" (title, language, level, status, challenges done out of total, last activity, "Open"; empty state with one "Create a lesson" button). No answer keys or private content. Wording: "Answer-key check" or "Practice only".
   - Follow-ups: the existing checkpoint cards and logic unchanged, plus a dot on the navbar Account item when one is due.
   - Settings: sign-in methods, guest-history claim card (only when applicable, existing logic), Trace account id inside a collapsed "For support" row, sign out, a privacy line. Don't build a display-name editor or leaderboard opt-out unless the backend contract has it; list both as questions.
5. States everywhere: skeleton, empty state with one primary button, error with retry and request id in a collapsed "Support details", and a clean signed-out prompt.

PART B: LEADERBOARD (`/leaderboard`)
Spec from my teammate (V1, keep it simple): global all-time board of all registered users; metric = number of DISTINCT verified problems completed; only verified completions count (attempts, drafts, unfinished, unverified never count); completing the same problem again counts once; sort by count descending; equal counts share the same rank; show rank, avatar, display name, and count; highlight the signed-in user; empty state when nobody has a verified completion.
1. Check the backend first (read-only): search `openapi.json` and `origin/main` for leaderboard endpoints. If one exists, wire it. If not, build against a PROPOSED contract in `src/lib/leaderboard/contracts.ts` (header "PROPOSED. Not a backend contract.") with a `LeaderboardClient`; the real client throws `EndpointNotAvailableError` until the endpoint exists and the page shows a friendly unavailable state. Demo data only in tests and behind the off-by-default flag `NEXT_PUBLIC_LEADERBOARD_DEMO=1` with a persistent "Demo data" label and fictional names. Proposed shape: cursor or offset pagination returning `{rank, user_id (opaque), display_name, avatar_url or avatar_id or null, verified_count, is_me}` plus a separate `me` object `{rank, verified_count}` (null when the user has no verified completion) and `generated_at`.
2. Page: requires sign-in (signed-out visitors get a short teaser and one "Sign in" button, no data). Header: pill, `SplitHeading` ("Leaderboard"), ONE line ("Ranked by verified problems completed. Only server-verified completions count."), and a small "How ranking works" disclosure (only verified, same problem counts once, ties share a rank).
3. "Your position" card (sticky on mobile): rank, avatar, name, count, and a neutral "Complete a Trace lesson to appear here" for users with no count. Show a "pass N people" nudge ONLY if the backend provides the number.
4. Top ranks: refined podium or highlight cards built from rank VALUES, not array positions (ties can put many users at rank 1; fall back to highlighted rows if layout gets awkward). List rows: rank in tabular numerals, avatar, truncated name with tooltip, count with a small bar scaled to the loaded page's top count. Tied users show the shared rank with an accessible "Rank 3, tied" label. Your row has an accent ring and a "You" badge, plus "Jump to my position" when it is on the loaded page.
5. Pagination via "Load more" without layout jump; don't render unbounded rows (cap or virtualize). Empty state with an original illustration, "No one is on the board yet", and a "Start a lesson" button; and a separate "You haven't completed a verified problem yet" state while others exist.
6. States: skeleton rows, error with retry and collapsed request id, rate-limited with retry hint, unavailable, unauthenticated, and a "Updated N minutes ago" indicator with a manual refresh (refetch on focus and every few minutes; stop when the tab is hidden).
7. Privacy and safety: display names and avatar URLs are untrusted. Render names as text only (handle HTML, emoji, RTL, zero-width, 500-character strings), load avatars only over HTTPS with `referrerPolicy="no-referrer"` and a fallback on error, empty name becomes "Trace learner", never show an email, never log other users' data. Add a footer line saying what the board shows (name and avatar) with a link to `/account`.
8. Integrations (minimal): a "Leaderboard" navbar item (desktop and mobile); the "Your rank" card on `/account`; one line on a Trace lesson's completion panel ("You're now #N with X verified problems") shown only after server-confirmed completion and only if the endpoint is available.

MOTION (transform and opacity only, no layout shift, reduced motion shows final states)
Heading reveal; staggered card and row entrance (cap at the first 12 rows); count-up on stats and counts (once, under 700ms); podium rise; one highlight pulse on your row; tab indicator slide; avatar dialog scale-and-fade; timeline line that draws into view; skeleton shimmer; subtle hover lift. A tasteful accent when your rank improved since your last visit, using only a locally stored last-seen rank that is never sent anywhere (omit if unavailable).

REQUIREMENTS DOCUMENT
Create or extend `docs/frontend-api-requests.md` with a "Leaderboard and account" section for my teammate: the proposed leaderboard endpoint and schemas, auth, errors, caching, rate limits; open questions: ranking type (competition 1,1,3 or dense 1,1,2), a stable tiebreak so pagination never skips or repeats, the `me` object, privacy (opt-out, default display name, avatar source and URL allowlist, name moderation), whether signed-out visitors can view it, what counts as one "problem" (a lesson or a challenge) and whether AI-created lessons ever count, page size, total count, freshness, and a backend `avatar_id` plus display-name field so other users can see chosen avatars. Include this flag for the team: with only two server-verified Trace lessons, scores will be 0, 1, or 2 and most users will tie until more verified problems exist.

TESTS AND VERIFICATION (don't claim what you didn't run)
- Vitest: custom lessons appear on `/account` and refresh after create, progress, and delete; stats hide when data is missing; tabs and URL sync; avatar is deterministic, never a letter, ignores ids outside the allowlist, and the picker saves, fails safely, and is keyboard operable; name fallback never uses the email; custom lessons never count as verified; every leaderboard state; ties show the same rank and tie label; the current-user highlight; "Your position" when off-page; load more without duplicates; hostile display names; no client-side sorting or rank computation; no request body ever carries a score; copy contains no "XP", "streak", or "points". Playwright (desktop and Pixel 7, repo test backend and a test-only fixture adapter for the leaderboard, mock provider transport only): navbar to Account and Leaderboard, tabs, avatar change persists after reload, custom lesson in history, follow-up flow, leaderboard load more and jump to my position, empty and signed-out states, keyboard-only path, no page errors, no horizontal overflow. Keep existing tests passing and don't weaken assertions.
- Run lint, typecheck, Vitest, `npm run api:types` drift check, Playwright, production build, and screenshots at 1440x900, 1024, 768, 390, 320 of every tab and state, the avatar dialog, and a many-ties leaderboard. Inspect them and fix alignment, wrapping, and contrast.

FINAL REPORT (under 35 lines): root cause of the missing custom lessons and its fix; per-area changes and files; what is real vs fixture vs demo-flag; the backend questions as one short plain message for my teammate (leaderboard endpoint, avatar field, display name source, opt-out); test and build results; and the exact `git add`, `git commit`, and `git push` commands for me to run myself.

The account redesign with avatars and the `/leaderboard` page are built on `feat/account-leaderboard`, frontend only; `backend/` is untouched. The leaderboard has no backend endpoint, so it shows "The leaderboard isn't open yet" until your teammate builds one.

**Why custom lessons were missing from `/account`:** the account page never requested them. It had no custom-lessons call or query at all, while My lessons reads `customLessonsApi.list()`.

- **The fix:** `/account`, `/learn` and My lessons now share one query key, and create, answer and delete send a change signal (also across tabs). The Playwright spec checks that a lesson created elsewhere appears on `/account`, and that deleting one on My lessons removes it there.
- **Trace stats:** a new account hides them until your first Trace lesson, because reading the journey earlier would create a learner and block the guest-history claim.

**What changed:**
- **Avatar:** 14 original abstract characters, the default is picked deterministically from your id (never a letter), and ids outside the allowlist are ignored. The picker is a keyboard-driven dialog. It saves `avatar_id` to your Supabase user metadata and falls back to localStorage with a visible message if that fails. It's used in the navbar, account header, leaderboard rows, and the completion line.
- **`/account`:** header card with "Change avatar", name (provider name, else "Trace learner", never the email), email, "Member since" and one primary action. Stats are real data only. Tabs synced to the URL: Overview, Learning history, Follow-ups (with a count badge when due), Settings.
- **`/leaderboard`:** sign-in teaser, a "How ranking works" note, a "Your position" card (sticky on mobile), rows with ties labelled "Rank 3, tied", "Load more" capped at 200 rows, and "Jump to my position". Names are rendered as plain text, avatars load only over HTTPS with no referrer, and nothing is sorted or ranked in the browser.
- **Navbar:** a Leaderboard item on desktop and mobile.
- **Rank on `/account` and on a lesson's completion panel:** shown only when the leaderboard returns real data.
- **Docs:** `docs/frontend-api-requests.md` has a new "Leaderboard and account" section with the proposed endpoint and your open questions.

**Real vs fixture vs demo:** the account, avatar and custom-lesson data are real. ## Error Type
Recoverable Error

## Error Message
Hydration failed because the server rendered HTML didn't match the client. As a result this tree will be regenerated on the client. This can happen if a SSR-ed Client Component used:

- A server/client branch `if (typeof window !== 'undefined')`.
- Variable input such as `Date.now()` or `Math.random()` which changes each time it's called.
- Date formatting in a user's locale which doesn't match the server.
- External changing data without sending a snapshot of it along with the HTML.
- Invalid HTML tag nesting.

It can also happen if the client has a browser extension installed which messes with the HTML before React loaded.

https://react.dev/link/hydration-mismatch

    at LoopStory (src\components\marketing\loop-story.tsx:76:11)
    at Home (src\app\page.tsx:20:9)

The Account overview is redesigned, but I haven't seen it rendered. It needs a signed-in session, so I couldn't screenshot it. `npx tsc --noEmit` and lint pass. Please open `/account` and check spacing, the charts and the glass look.

**New overview layout**, loosely following your dashboard reference:
- **Profile card:** now light glass over a soft warm, rose and teal glow. The glass uses a modest blur and is used only on the profile card and the dashboard cards.
- **Next up row:** "Continue where you left off" and your leaderboard rank sit side by side. A due follow-up still appears above them when there is one.
- **Stat tiles:** the same five numbers, restyled as glass cards.
- **Trace lessons:** a ring showing lessons completed out of the total, with a progress bar and status for each lesson.
- **Attempts and hints:** paired bars per lesson, with the counts above each bar.
- **Activity:** a column per day for the last 14 days, counting lessons you started, completed or created.
- **Lessons you created:** a ring of completed out of created, plus links to your latest three. If you have none, it links to "Create a lesson".
- **Recent activity:** the same timeline as before, below the grid.

All of it draws only from your saved sessions and created lessons, with no invented numbers and no mastery score. Each chart has a text summary for screen readers.

**Animation:** bars grow, rings draw in and progress bars fill when the page loads. With reduced motion they appear in their final state. Below 1024px the cards stack in one column.

The other two tabs (Learning history and Settings) are unchanged. I added one new file, `account-charts.tsx`, and changed `account-dashboard.tsx` and `globals.css`.

The navbar now has two states, and I checked both in a screenshot at 1440px. It applies to every page that uses the shared header.

- **At the top of the page:** the bar hangs flush from the top edge of the page canvas like a tab, with square top corners, rounded bottom corners and no top border. It's the same white bar, so it reads as part of the page. It only fully works on a light canvas, so on pages with a coloured top it will look like the reference's white notch.
- **After you scroll past about 40px:** it lifts into the floating rounded bar with its border and deeper shadow. The change takes about 0.4s: the bar moves down, the corners round and the shadow grows.
- **Page layout:** the header's size doesn't change between the two states, so nothing below it jumps. With reduced motion the switch is instant.

I moved the bar's border, radius and base shadow out of the markup in `site-header.tsx` and into `globals.css`, so one set of rules controls both states. `npx tsc --noEmit` and lint pass. The mobile layout is not checked.

I rebuilt the Trace lesson pages to match the AI-created lessons and the landing demo. I haven't loaded them in a browser, because they need a signed-in session and the backend, so please try both lessons end to end. `npx tsc --noEmit` and lint pass. I did not run or update any tests, so tests that mention the old docked tutor will fail.

- **Same page:** both Trace lessons now use the site navbar, the calm background and the same header as AI-created lessons: back link, tags, title and objective. The old breadcrumb bar is gone, and I deleted `lesson-top-bar.tsx`.
- **Same flow:** the lesson is now one card with a Read, Predict, Modify, Verify, Revisit stepper. Code is on the left and one step at a time on the right, like the landing demo and your custom lessons.
  - **Read:** you press "I've read it" and the session starts.
  - **Predict:** the prediction form. Your answer locks and can't be changed.
  - **Modify:** your locked prediction, the in-code edit, the check button, and the fixture hints ("Fixture guidance").
  - **Verify:** the server's evidence and attempt history. "Try another change" takes you back to Modify.
  - **Revisit:** the follow-ups already shown below the lesson. In the stepper it only lights up once the lesson is complete.
  - **Backend:** attempts, hints, prediction locking and verification all still use the existing backend. I only changed the layout.
- **Tutor at the bottom:** the docked side panel is gone. Trace lessons now use the same bottom "Lesson tutor" bar that opens a drawer, like custom lessons.
- **Stepper labels:** the middle step is now "Modify" in both Trace and AI-created lessons, to match the demo. AI-created lessons also gained the "Revisit" step in the stepper.

Two differences remain on purpose. Trace lessons are checked by running the code on a server ("Checked on a real server"), while custom lessons are checked against an answer key. And Trace hints come from the saved fixture endpoint, while custom hints come from the lesson itself.

Both kinds of lesson now open on Predict, like the demo, with no separate "I've read it" step. I haven't tried either in a browser, because they need a signed-in session and the backend, so please run through one of each. `npx tsc --noEmit` and lint pass, and I did not run or update tests.

- **AI-created lessons:** the Read panel and its "I've read it" button are gone. A challenge opens with the code on the left and the Predict step on the right, and the stepper shows Read as already done. After that it works as before: lock the answer, then Modify (edit a line in the code and pick the answer), then Verify, with a wrong answer sending you back to Modify.
- **Trace lessons:** they also open straight on Predict. Pressing "Lock prediction" starts the session first, if there isn't one yet, and then saves your prediction. This also covers the case where you left an earlier attempt: locking a new prediction starts a new one. The Read step now only shows if the transfer lesson is still locked or a saved session is being restored.

I left out the demo's "observe" step, where it shows the original code's result before you modify. Trace lessons have no real run of the original code to show. AI-created lessons are checked against an answer key, so showing the result first would give the answer away.

I rebuilt both kinds of lesson on the same window as the interactive demo, but I haven't seen it rendered, because the lesson pages need a signed-in session and the backend. `npx tsc --noEmit`, lint and a production build all pass, and I did not run or update any tests. Please open one AI-created lesson and one Trace lesson and compare them side by side with the demo.

- **Shared window:** the title bar has the Trace mark, "Lessons / title", and the Read, Predict, Modify, Verify, Revisit rail with check marks and a pill on the current step. It also has the checking badge on the right, with the code on the left and one step at a time on the right. The plain-language note runs along the bottom.
- **Predict:** the question, radio cards with a letter chip (Trace lessons show a one-line detail instead), an optional "why", and a "Lock prediction" button with "Locked predictions can't be changed."
- **Modify:** the locked prediction card, then the edit inside the code ("type your line" in AI-created lessons, and the highlighted key in Trace lessons). Below that are "Check my change" with the Enter hint, "Show a hint" with a yellow hint box, and a red "Not yet ·" box for errors.
- **Verify:** evidence cards that appear one after another, a "Why it happened" list, and a "This attempt" box with badges such as Prediction correct or differed, Hint used or No hint, and Matched on first try. After that comes "Try another change" or the completion panel.

What differs from the demo:
- **No "Run it" step:** the demo's "Show sample result" step has no equivalent. A real lesson has no sample run of the original code to reveal. AI-created lessons are checked against an answer key, so showing the answer first would give it away.
- **Evidence tag:** the cards are tagged with their real source ("Server" or "Lesson") instead of "Sample".
- **Revisit:** it is only a note once a lesson is complete. A real follow-up opens later on your Account page.

I also added a flat code-pane option to `CodeWindow`. The new shared pieces are in `lesson-window.tsx`, and I restyled `forms.tsx`, `evidence.tsx`, `challenge-loop.tsx` and `workspace.tsx` around them. The attempts, hints and verification still go through the existing backend calls unchanged.
